Our province, a traditionally important agricultural trade centre, is also an important industrial centre that extracts raw material from agriculture. Mold, olive oil, pepper, grain products, and soap are our main industrial products. In the period of the EC PARTI governments, as in all of Turkey, in our province, we built many dams and ponds to allow our farmers to obtain more and more quality products. We've solved the need for our city's drinking water with the dams we've built. In our province, which is also the center of attraction, as well as our agricultural activities, 34 factories in our organised industrial region are under construction. When the construction of these factories is completed, there will be employment for about 5,000 others. We are also continuing our work for our current organised industrial region's third expansion area, capable of 44 factories. After its infrastructure is completed, our province will take a serious step towards becoming an important industrial city as well as becoming an agricultural city. The Church is a richer province in terms of historical and architectural works. There are many historical sites, such as the Ottoman Empire and the Ottoman ancient mosques, fountains, historical churches, synagogues, soaphouses. Restorations of these structures have been restored and opened to the service of our people. The Oylum Höyüyük, located in our village of Kilis-Oylum, is one of the largest mounds of the Middle East, as well as a central feature of the cultures of Anatolia, Syria and Mesopotamia. Archaeological excavations on the mound, considered the oldest church settlement, also shed light on the region's history of 5,500 years. Also found during excavations in the mound, the four, five hundred-year-old olive core is proof that the main source of the olive is the Church. Our province is now known to be home to our brothers and sisters who have defected to our country because of the civil war in Syria, which we live in the south. The capital of peace, tolerance, hospitality, the capital city of central city Kilis hosts more Syrian refugees than its own population during the time when many Western countries have closed their borders and tried every way to prevent them from coming. The beautiful and main city of our Southeast Anatolian region, which still has traces of its culture, its works, its flavors, has become much more habitable today with its roads, dams, university, substructure, and substructure than ever before.

Malfunction has been described differently lately. Different definitions have been made, but the definition accepted today is referred to as loss of any function of the body, which is disabled if any function has been lost. Therefore, the definition and scope of disability has expanded. Of course, disability is common in the world as well as in our country. Turkey's studies indicate that 12 per cent of the population is disabled. As it turns out, it's already very common, and in cases of extreme disability, we have a situation that concerns far more people than one person's family is affected by. As the National Movement Party, we see the disabled as equal citizens, as stated in Article 10 of our Constitution, that the disabled have equal rights in all matters. In addition, we, the National Movement Party, are in favor of all the disabled living independent and dependent on no one, and we state the need for arrangements to be made. The disability is made up of various reasons. Of course, we need to prevent disability. Some disability can happen for natural reasons, for genetic reasons. Of course, prenatal genetic counseling is crucial to prevent genetic disability. Another issue may arise when your mother suffers from the illness during pregnancy. Early diagnosis is vital for preventing and for all disability. The sooner we diagnose it, the more successful it is to treat it and maintain it. After the birth, a disability may arise. In particular, diseases or accidents cause disability. It is also important to prevent such diseases or accidents, especially car accidents, and workplace accidents. The disabled have many problems; they have education problems, health problems, other than that, access issues and employment problems. He has to be able to access anything that's disabled independently. It is the duty of municipalities to make arrangements for this within the city, as you know, and municipalities must organise it. It is especially important to make arrangements for the transportation of disabled persons in public transportation or on roads. We also need to make facilitating arrangements for the access of disabled persons in public institutions, especially in schools and health institutions. The disabled have a lot of problems with education. They need special training. All disabled persons must be included in education. Specially, private education needs to be expanded and easy access. In terms of health care, of course, the disabled have very important problems. They need more health care, and it is important that the treatments of those who are disabled in this greater health care include repayment of all equipment for the disabled. Because in the comfort of their lives, we also need to provide that. There is a major problem with the employment of disabled persons: they cannot be employed enough. In institutions with more than 50% employees, public institutions have 4 per cent quotas and private institutions have 3 per cent quotas, but 20,000 are known to be available to the disabled in 2020. In particular, 17,000 quotas are available in private institutions. It is very important to put them in place, to employ them. There is a problem with the disabled KPS. We also recommend that the disabled KPSS (KPSS) be in separate forms of examinations based on their disability status. We don't think we should put every handicapped person in the same boat.

Economic Development and Co-operation Organization was signed in November 2011 to prevent tax evasion, black money and financing of terrorism in the OECD. The Agreement on Automated Exchange of Financial Account Information, which sets the implementation of the contract, was signed in 2017, with the signature of President Erdogan on 31 December 2019. tbmm-2020-12-02sit04spe0341par0044	Mr. President, I have made a pledge on behalf of the Nationalist Movement Party Group over the establishment of the Turkish Environment Agency and the proposal for the amendment to some Laws. I hereby salute our Veteran Assembly and our beloved nation. Dear lawmakers, the problems that affect the world we live in today are no doubt about environmental problems. With environmental problems beginning to influence and threaten mankind in a negative way, international public opinion has made various agreements to solve environmental problems, to leave a habitable world for future generations, and to start awareness studies. Our country also strives to fulfill its responsibilities under international agreements, and through relevant institutions and organisations, it works to protect the environment. In the past year of law enforcement, arrangements have been made for Environment Law No. 2872, which we have all contributed to, and is commonly known as the Law on Postage, under the use of plastic bags and the Zero Waste Project. The work was indeed a successful arrangement, and its positive effects were soon seen. Today, the environmental agency we're going to talk about is an institution that will be established and run for the same purpose. A environmental agency is established to avoid pollution, to contribute to the protection and development of green areas, to establish a national-scale deposit management system, to operate, monitor and control activities. In addition, there are regulations in our country on the creation of legal infrastructure related to electricity, which has recently been widely used but has no legal infrastructure, and on bicycles, and on increasing the number of bicycle routes and promoting our people to sports. These regulations aim to include in time the deposit management system for products, such as everyday plastics, battery waste, electronics, battery waste, and amplification waste, which have negatively affected the environment. We, the National Movement Party, feel that this institution will be useful to work on solving environmental problems, and we find it valuable to contribute to any positive work on the environment. Dear lawmakers have an unlimited relationship with the environment in which humans are born and grow and live. There is no doubt a balance in this relationship. In the 21st century, we would not have been mistaken if we said it was a century in the history of the earth, when this balance was largely disturbed, when mankind faced environmental problems that had never been encountered before. Natural resources, such as air, water, and soil that sustain life on Earth, are being contaminated or consumed by frightening levels. In the process of rapidly growing populations, agriculture, forests with ecological balance insurance, and toxic gases and waste that threaten life are constantly increasing around the world. The environmental problems began to talk internationally in the 70s and 80s, and the United Nations Environment and Human Conference met in Stockholm in 1972, so for the first time, environmental problems were discussed globally. Today, the world's population is based on 8 billion. With the growing population, demand has increased, consumption has increased, and the balance that I've just described in the relationship between nature and mankind has begun to deteriorate against nature. Every year, 2.1 billion tonnes of garbage are produced worldwide, and only 16 percent of them are recycled. Forty-six percent of the garbage, unfortunately, is irreversibly thrown out. The extent of the danger will be better understood, given the use of 88 billion disposable pet bottles per year only of the beverage manufacturer Coca Cola. China, the United States, India, Russia, Japan, and Germany are the most polluting countries in the world, based on carbon emissions. These countries are the most responsible countries for the world's environmental problems, according to the logic. Our country is the 15th place in carbon emissions. In our country, 25 million tonnes of household waste appear to be 1.2 million tonnes of industrial waste; 100,000 tons of medical waste and 530,000 tons of e-accession per year. The recycling sector in our country is growing and growing with the contributions of private sectors and municipalities, and in parallel, the number of licensed investors in the recycling sector is increasing day by day. It is now a necessity to quickly implement recycling projects in today's world, where consumption is reckless. Recycling waste, benefiting from waste is also a great need for our economy. With the recycling in our country, the Zero Waste projects are progressing with good intentions, but we need to make a little more effort to collect waste, separate waste, and make our citizens aware of it. In particular, disintegrating disposable plastics, used masks, disinfectant cans pose great risks in terms of pollution during the pandemic period. By avoiding the virus, we need to be clear that we should not invite other viruses, and we need to speed up awareness studies. Mr. President, valuable lawmakers, debates on matters of vital importance to our future, such as environmental awareness and the protection of nature, are being made in the wrong plane as we see it. This is that almost everyone who talks about the environment is taking references and showing the Western world, and we find it incomplete and wrong because the negatives are ignored as they speak of the positive work of the West. The fact that recycling rates have increased in Western countries doesn't change the fact that they've destroyed and polluted our world so far. The idea of the West is based on the idea that respect for nature prevents it from ruling. For example, Francis Bacon and Descartes, one of the cornerstones of Western thought, viewed man as the master and owner of nature, and in this understanding that nature and culture are separated by precise lines, nature is seen as an area to be controlled and digested. Today, this mentality is trying to confess. The idea that destroys air, water, soil, has developed environmental rhetoric in recent years, creating and perceiving various public relations studies. Despite our shortcomings today, we exploit the world and destroy natural resources, and then we destroy it, and you don't. There's no reason for us to take direction from those who try to manipulate us. In Turkey's cultural acquish and civilization, the environment and nature are important. What is it like to look at nature, the environment, in our culture, and in our civilization? We can immediately say that perhaps the most important thing that separates Turkish culture from Western culture is human nature. In Turks, nature is part of culture itself; it is possible to see it in all our written, verbal sources, such as trees. The tree is a cultural element in the Turks. Turkish culture, tree architecture and urbanism, in other words, reconciles nature with civilization. I've been in this study for 11 months, and there's very clear data that this study has shown us. So today in Turkey, 32 million tons of waste per year is produced, and 25 per cent of them are packed to drink. So our paper, metal, glass, and plastic beverage wrappers are now produced 20 billion copies, and all solid waste actually produces 25 percent of its volume. And the main issue here is, why does Turkey need the deposit management system? We should actually collect recycleable waste separately between ourselves. We don't get enough of these when we collect mixed products. But if we could collect plastic waste, glass waste, metal waste separately, even use them as raw materials before they become waste, we'd get a much more effective recycling. Therefore, we find it very important that the deposit management system be carried out for the collection of plastic bottles, glass bottles, metal boxes, all the beverage packaging separately. It's in the industry because we know it's imported granite for the production of plastic bottles, and we know it's imported sand for the production of glass bottles. But we can produce these bottles directly from a broken glass or a plastic bottle drop, and we can get rid of five times the energy costs here. So, through the Security Management System, we aim to recycle 90 per cent of 20 billion beverage packaging within three years, and we plan to contribute $2 billion per year to Turkey's economy, with the cost reduction in energy and imports. Again, if I were to mention the changes in the Environmental Law, which is the second part of our law, especially in the principle section, we have dropped very important notes. One of them is the spread of zero waste, and the other is the fight against climate change. Again, we place very important points in principles such as the spread of motor or electric or environmental transportation vehicles and encouraging them and reducing the disposable materials that we care about very much, and we desire more effective work at this point in the coming period. Again, we allow these companies to co-optify among themselves so that they can manage wastewaters more effectively, related to waste water management. See, here's the problem: some of the waste collected separately in its source can sometimes be removed from that quality by local governments. So we also open the way for separate waste collected in its source to be delivered directly to recycling facilities. So here, buildings such as universities, websites, hotels, restaurants, cafes, and malls will be able to collect their own waste and give it directly to the recycling facilities. And we think that's very important. Again, all activities without environmental permits and licenses will be suspended indefinitely. Executive fines are also being greatly increased in terms of increasing deterrentism. Turkey will receive a fine of a thousand lira to those who are polluting the environment with any waste anywhere from now on if this offer becomes legal. Waste motor oil is a title I care about a lot. Turkey produces 400,000 tonnes of motor oil per year, making 200,000 tons of them, or half of them waste. We can only recycle 20,000 tons of this 200,000 tons of waste engine oil. Today, 180,000 tons of waste engine oil is unfortunately mixed or burned, and we also know that we can actually get base oils from these waste motor oils, which we call oil. Unfortunately, in the industry today, these base oils can be imported from abroad. Now, with this arrangement, we're going to save 180,000 tons of waste motor oil from going around with our proposal of the law, and we're going to be contributing to the economy by recycling it. Again, we have voluntarily added the environmental tag system to the Environmental Law, which is found in European Union standards. The third part of the law is related to environmental transports, bicycle routes and e-directory services. I'd like to express here that I'm very important, both as an Iconian MP and as a young member of the nation, because Iconia is the 2nd city in the world today with the longest bicycle path since New York. Yeah, you didn't hear wrong, there's 500 miles of bike routes in Iconya. Therefore, we personally know how important bicycle paths are. Again, the Conium Metropolitan Municipality has first prepared a bicycle master plan for its transport master plan and has implemented it. So, we do homework on building bicycle roads and making noise barriers on the roads because we know how important these bicycle paths are to environmental transports in terms of reducing carbon emissions. Again, we're bringing the local authorities to include bicycle master plans in their transportation plans to 30 major city municipalities. Again, at the intersection of the routes used by bicycle roads and normal vehicles, we give the transcendence to e-squaters and bicycles and block the bike routes to vehicles and foot traffic to ensure traffic safety. Again, there are certain arrangements for these e-squaters, which young people are frequently sought for as a young MP. In fact, in our proposal of this law, we put these electric scooters on a legal basis, we make a definition, and we say that E-Skuters can't do more than 25 kilometers per hour. Again, the age of use has been set at 15, no driver's license required, which is crucial for the safety of traffic. Again, only personal cargo can be carried and one person can ride a scooter, so that's how we arranged it. And for the incentive of these environmental transports, we've determined from the bottom tariffs these scooter companies will pay local governments for their invasion, 16 cents a day, and we believe that with these arrangements, these transports will become widespread and carbon emissions, greenhouse gas emissions, hopefully, will be reduced. Here, of course, under the roof of this veteran Congress, I believe all of our MPs are environmentally sensitive. Yes, it's been expressed before, the environment is really a political issue. Leaving a clean environment for our future generations is something we should all be sensitive about, and I have no doubt about it. In this regard, we've done very important studies before, with the law called the plastic bag law, we've managed to reduce the use of plastic bags by 80 percent per year by reducing the use of plastic bags from 440 to 90. We've all done this together. I believe that with today's law proposal, we'll be taking a very good step forward for our environment, for our future. I salute our coven and our beloved nation. Today, environmental problems are undoubtedly at the head of problems facing mankind and its effects are increasing day by day. At first, the problems we face on a local-based, narrow scale have grown over time and its effects have reached global size. Climate change, air pollution, plastic waste islands that surround the oceans, garbage heaps on land are some of the problems that want solutions. Therefore, we need to act in common rather than individually at the point of finding solutions. This pandemic process that we've been through has shown us how much damage we've done to our world. You'll remember, during the first period of the outbreak, strict measures were taken in many countries, with almost 90 percent of the world's mobility reduced. And yet, our air has been cleared, and far away has become visible. We can see that the world's public has been leaning towards these important problems in the last 50 years. However, in our country, we observe that environmental arrangements, effective steps, which are a very dynamic issue, have only been taken in the last 20 years. Regular storage of wastes, widespread recycling activities, so that in the early 2000s, investments in facilities known as 10-20 increased greatly by legal regulations and improved policies, reaching the 3-4 thousand today. In addition, these facilities provide employment potential for tens of thousands of people. Again, we have seen serious developments in many areas, such as decontamination of wastewaters and reuse of water supply, improving air quality, and increasing in protected areas. See, our Environmental Law is nearly 40 years old, but we see that it has been subjected to extensive revisions twice in 2006 and 2018, and has had minor adjustments before. But we define the environment as preventative medicine, which I think should be considered above-politics. From this point of view, I stress that our country's resources are more rational and efficient and that we have both individual and social responsibilities. Precious Regents, the change in the Environmental Law in 2018 was based on waste management. We can say that the arrangements in particular have been a revolution in waste management in our country. The arrangement paid for plastic bags, which reduced the use of bags by 80 percent. It also means saving on raw materials. The recycling share application created the financial infrastructure of the zero waste system. Now, we have a mandatory deposit on our agenda that has achieved 90 per cent and above recycling success in countries that have been implemented abroad. The mandatory deposit aims to bring life to a deposit application, an important component of the system. Respectfully, lawmakers are considered three main issues in the current proposal. The first is the establishment of the Environment Agency, the second is the spread of zero waste, and finally, the widespread spread of environmentally friendly bicycle transport. The Environment Agency, which will be offered, will have many activities, and the first and foremost of them will be to manage the Securities/Responsion System. The deposit is a mechanism that allows waste to be collected effectively. The application has a very simple function in practice. The price of packaging is reflected in the product, and when the packaging is returned, the fee is returned. It's not a financial burden to the citizen. In 2002, the recycling rate of water solid waste was 11%, and now the recycling rate of solid waste was 13%. In our 2023 goals, with the establishment of this agency, we aim at 35 percent. On the other hand, Mr. Ergun told the Agency that the Ministry of Environment and Urbanism in the Environmental Law is in charge. We also discussed this in the Commission, which is clearly stated in Article 12 of the Environmental Law 2872, under the provisions of the Environmental Law, the Ministry of Environment and Urbanism. It has also been said that the Ministry of Environment and Urbanism will determine the procedure and the basis for the audits. Under Law 2872, the Ministry has been granted the right to hand over the Ministry's control authority to the following institutions. What institutions are these? The state's private administrations, the mayors established environmental control units, the Undersecretary of Maritime, the Coast Guard Command, the Coast Guard, and now, I believe, have come to a motion under the Law Law Code 2918 -- I would like to say to the Security General Directorate -- that in this scope, it is also considered to include the European Environment Agency among the institutions referred to as Article 14 of the Law Proposal. The goal of handing over control authority to Turkey's Environment Agency is to increase the control activity, as in the institutions that have been transferred control authority. So the event under control is to increase and impose sanctions on those who violate environmental law laws. As we say above, the Constitution does not violate the Constitution because of the fact that the 14th Article does not have a different verdict as well as the institutions in which control authority is handed over, and that the purpose is to increase the control activity in the Constitution according to the law. On the other hand, Mr. Izwar, our Van MP, has a question that there will be an incursion fee from electric scooter operators shared by Turkey's Environment Agency, and that is why shared electricity scooters cannot be popularized. First of all, there is no tuition from electric scooter operators shared by Turkey's Environment Agency. The fee for the invasion from shared electric scooter operators is organised by the City's Finance Act 2464 and is collected by municipalities in the current situation. The amendment to the Law Proposal aims to increase the use of electric scooters and to ensure unity in the implementation, provided that the tuition fees for the occupation are calculated by the minimum tariff in the Law of City revenues (2464). Thank you, Mr. President.
